    *   Constraint: Use canonical graph names for entities (provided: `Charlie Hunnam`).

    *   Goal: Identify the movie(s) featuring Charlie Hunnam and determine which one has the most recent release date based on retrieved evidence.

2.  **Stage A — Understand the Question:**
    *   **Requested slot:** `movie` (specifically, the title/name of the movie).
    *   **Explicit entities:** `Charlie Hunnam`.
    *   **Supporting conditions:** "featuring" (implies acting role), "released most recently" (requires temporal comparison/ordering).
    *   **Answer Variable:** `?movie` (the specific film entity).
    *   **Answer Type:** `movie` (or `film`).

3.  **Stage B — Build Entity-Centered Evidence:**
    *   **Entity:** `Charlie Hunnam`.
    *   **Goal:** Find movies he appeared in, then find their release dates, then compare to find the most recent.
    *   **Subgraph Structure:**
        *   Anchor: `Charlie Hunnam`.
        *   Fact 1: Retrieve movies where this person acted/featured.
        *   Fact 2: Retrieve release dates for those movies.
        *   Comparison: The "most recently" constraint is handled during Answer Analysis (comparing dates from retrieved bindings), not as a separate graph fact (per Rule #13: Do NOT create graph facts for comparisons/rankings).
    *   **Plan Details:**
        *   `entities`: Charlie Hunnam
        *   `answer`: ?movie
        *   `answer_type`: movie
        *   `sg1.anchor`: Charlie Hunnam
        *   `sg1.f1`: Charlie Hunnam | which movies did this person act in | ?movie
        *   `sg1.f2`: ?movie | when was this movie released | ?date
            *   *Note:* I need to check if there's a direct relation for release date or if it goes through an event node. Usually, Freebase has `release_date` or similar. Let's assume standard retrieval path: Person -> Film -> Release Date.

            *   Plan structure:

                *   sg1.f2: ?movie | when was this movie released | ?date
            *   This retrieves both the set of movies and their dates. The analysis will pick the movie with the max date.

4.  **Refining the Plan:**

    *   Answer Type: `movie`

    *   Variables: `?movie`, `?date`.

    answer_type: movie

    sg1.anchor: Charlie Hunnam

Thinking Process:

1.  **Analyze the Request:**
    *   Input Question: "Which country borders France and has a per capita emissions rate per metric ton is 10.126703?"
    *   Constraint: Use the provided canonical graph names for entities (currently only "France" is explicitly given as an anchor in the prompt's entity list, though the question implies searching for a country).
    *   Task: Create a plan (`tool: plan`) to answer the question using the SEQ Agent V2.3 protocol.
    *   Protocol Requirements:
        *   Stage A: Understand the question (requested slot, explicit entities, supporting conditions).
        *   Stage B: Build entity-centered evidence (subgraphs, facts, variables).
        *   Format: Flat text, no JSON.
        *   Principles: Semantic-Target-First, Entity-Centered Evidence KGQA.
        *   Answer Variable: Must represent the requested semantic slot ("country").
        *   Entities: Must include all explicit named entities from the question ("France").
        *   Subgraphs: Each explicit entity anchors its own subgraph.
        *   Facts: Must connect entities to the answer variable or constrain it.
        *   Relations: Must be retrieved via `retrieve_relations` later, but planned here.

2.  **Stage A — Understand the Question:**
    *   **Requested Slot:** "Which country..." -> The answer must be a `country`.
    *   **Explicit Entities:** "France". (The question asks for a country that *borders* France).
    *   **Supporting Conditions:**
        1.  Borders France.
        2.  Has a per capita emissions rate per metric ton = 10.126703.
    *   **Semantic Interpretation:** I need to find a country that satisfies two conditions relative to France or independently.
        *   Condition 1: Neighbor of France.
        *   Condition 2: Specific emissions value.
    *   **Answer Variable:** `?country` (type: country).

3.  **Stage B — Build Entity-Centered Evidence:**
    *   **Anchor:** "France".
    *   **Goal:** Find countries bordering France AND matching the emissions criteria.
    *   **Subgraph Strategy:**
        *   Start with "France".
        *   Retrieve countries bordering France. This gives a set of candidate countries.
        *   For each candidate country, check if it has the specific emissions rate.
    *   **Fact Construction:**
        *   `sg1.anchor`: France
        *   `sg1.f1`: France | which countries border this country | ?country
        *   `sg1.f2`: ?country | what is this country's per capita emissions rate per metric ton | ?emissions_rate
        *   Wait, the second fact needs to filter by the specific value. In KGQA planning, we usually retrieve the relation first. If the graph stores the value, we retrieve it. Then we compare at analysis time.
        *   Alternatively, can we search for countries with that specific value directly? Usually, Freebase/Graph structures store attributes on nodes. So retrieving the attribute on the neighbor node is the standard path.
        *   Plan structure:
            *   Anchor: France.
            *   Fact 1: Get neighbors (countries).
            *   Fact 2: Get emissions data for those neighbors.
    *   **Variables:**
        *   `?country` (Answer variable).
        *   `?emissions_rate` (Constraint variable).
    *   **Entities:** France.

4.  **Refining the Plan:**
    *   Does the question imply there might be multiple countries? Yes ("Which country...").
    *   Is "France" the only explicit entity? Yes.
    *   Do I need a second anchor? No, because the condition "borders France" defines the search space starting from France. The emissions constraint is applied to the results of that search.
    *   However, sometimes it's safer to treat the specific value as a potential anchor if the graph supports querying by value, but standard KGQA usually retrieves relations from known entities. Since "France" is the only named entity provided as an anchor in the prompt context ("Question entities... - France"), I must start there.

    *   So, `entities: France`.
    *   `answer: ?country`.

For EACH OPEN fact: call retrieve_relations to find question-RELEVANT relations (all that match, typically ≤3, max 5 — not just the single best), then retrieve_subgraph with ALL of them together. After each retrieve_subgraph, close the fact with ONE of three checkpoints: resolve it `- fid ✓ ?var = v1 | v2`; close it EMPTY `- fid ✗ empty` when no candidate relation advances it; close it MOOT `[fid ✗ moot]` when earlier evidence already bound its target. MULTI-TREE (question names SEVERAL entities): prefer a separate anchor view per entity when the entities provide independent constraints, and let the system JOIN them (an entity reachable from both sides intersects the candidate sets); an entity that adds no independent constraint does not need its own tree. For later facts continuing the SAME tree, just pass the ANCHOR or any retrieved entity + the new relation — the system walks the full chain from the root automatically.
